# World History Unit and Lesson Map

**Status:** Approved course architecture (2026-08-03)  
**Primary curriculum:** Savvas Realize: World History Interactive Florida  
**Florida course:** World History 2109310

## Course Structure Summary

| Unit | Unit Title | Unit Purpose | Lesson Count | Unit Pretest | Unit Assessment |
|---|---|---|---:|---|---|
| Unit 1 | Historical Thinking, Geography, and Medieval Foundations | Build historical inquiry, map skills, source analysis, and explain the transition from the Roman world to medieval societies. | 8 | Required | Required |
| Unit 2 | Islamic Civilizations, African Kingdoms, and American Civilizations | Analyze Islamic expansion, African empires, and major American civilizations through cultural, political, economic, and geographic evidence. | 8 | Required | Required |
| Unit 3 | Japan, Renaissance, Reformation, Scientific Revolution, and Exploration | Connect medieval Japan, Renaissance creativity, religious reform, scientific change, exploration, colonization, and forced labor systems. | 8 | Required | Required |
| Unit 4 | Absolutism, Enlightenment, Revolutions, Industrialization, and Imperialism | Analyze political authority, Enlightenment ideas, revolutions, industrialization, reform, nationalism, and imperialism. | 8 | Required | Required |
| Unit 5 | World War I, Interwar Crisis, World War II, and the Holocaust | Examine global conflict, authoritarianism, the Holocaust, Allied strategy, atomic warfare, and the consequences of World War II. | 8 | Required | Required |
| Unit 6 | Cold War, Decolonization, Globalization, Genocide, and Contemporary Issues | Analyze Cold War systems, decolonization, conflict, globalization, terrorism, pandemics, science, and responses to genocide. | 8 | Required | Required |

## Total Course Counts

| Item | Count |
|---|---:|
| Units | 6 |
| Lessons | 48 |
| Unit Pretests | 6 |
| Notebook Evidence Submissions | 48 |
| Checkpoints | 48 |
| Lesson or Cluster Quizzes | 42 |
| Unit Assessments | 6 |
| Projects/DBQs/Performance Tasks | At least 6 cumulative synthesis products, with approved Savvas Quest Inquiry options |

## Governing Architecture Rules

- Six units and eight lessons per unit are retained because the verified standards and Savvas sources form six coherent chronological clusters, each with seven instructional clusters and one synthesis lesson.
- The count is an academic result, not a fixed template.
- Unit 1 establishes historical inquiry, chronology, geography, source analysis, and evidence writing.
- Every unit begins with an ungraded Unit Pretest and ends with a cumulative synthesis containing the Unit Assessment.
- Savvas provides Lesson Quizzes and editable quizzes for instructional sources and Topic Assessments for full topics; only aligned questions and required stimuli may be transferred.
- Exact Savvas titles, begin/stop points, source dependencies, and student actions are required during production.
- No teacher-only file or protected answer may be exposed.
- COURSE_OVERVIEW.md, COURSE_CROSSWALK.md, and this file jointly govern Moodle production.

# Unit 1 — Historical Thinking, Geography, and Medieval Foundations

## Unit Purpose

Build historical inquiry, map skills, source analysis, and explain the transition from the Roman world to medieval societies.

## Foundational Role

Unit 1 explicitly teaches historical inquiry, chronology, geography, source evaluation, corroboration, map reading, and evidence-based explanation before applying those practices to Byzantine and medieval societies. Savvas Topic 0 is used selectively for prior-learning support; it does not add off-standard ancient-history mastery requirements.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Explain the unit’s major historical developments with accurate chronology and vocabulary.
2. Analyze required sources, maps, timelines, visuals, and data.
3. Construct evidence-based explanations and demonstrate mastery in the Unit 1 synthesis and assessment.

# Unit 1, Lesson 1 — Historical Inquiry, Timelines, and Cause and Effect

## Lesson Identification

| Field | Requirement |
|---|---|
| MLA Lesson ID | WH-U1-L1 |
| Savvas Source | Florida Standards and Assessment Overview; Topic 0 Opener; Topic 0 Lessons 1-5 source excerpts and timelines |
| Estimated Scope | One standards-aligned instructional cluster |
| Prerequisite Knowledge | Prior course and unit lessons; readiness evidence from Unit 1 Pretest |
| Leads To | Unit 1, Lesson 2 |

## Lesson Purpose

Students learn how historians move from evidence to defensible explanation.

## Learning Objectives

By the end of the lesson, students will be able to:

1. Explain the central historical developments and vocabulary.
2. Analyze the required source, map, timeline, chart, or comparison evidence.
3. Construct an accurate evidence-based historical explanation aligned to SS.912.W.1.1, SS.912.W.1.2, SS.912.W.1.3, SS.912.W.1.4, SS.912.W.1.5, SS.912.W.1.6.

## Standards

| Standard | Instructional Role | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| SS.912.W.1.1, SS.912.W.1.2, SS.912.W.1.3, SS.912.W.1.4, SS.912.W.1.5, SS.912.W.1.6 | Introduced, practiced, reinforced, mastered, or assessed as specified in COURSE_CROSSWALK.md | Notebook Evidence; Checkpoint; approved Savvas Lesson or cluster quiz |

## Lesson Evidence Summary

### Notebook Evidence

Students must include:

1. Accurate vocabulary, chronology, geography, or context notes.
2. The required timeline/source-corroboration table.
3. A source-supported explanation showing historical reasoning.

### Checkpoint Evidence

Students must submit:

1. A completed timeline/source-corroboration table.
2. A defensible claim or explanation.
3. Relevant evidence and reasoning labeled WH-U1-L1.

### Other Required Evidence

- Complete the exact assigned Savvas resources and aligned Lesson or cluster quiz.

## Lesson Moodle Page Sequence

1. Lesson Overview and Learning Objectives
2. Vocabulary, Context, and Chronology
3. Savvas eText and Required Source/Media
4. Guided Source, Map, Timeline, or Data Analysis
5. Independent Historical Application and Notebook Task
6. Checkpoint and Lesson/Cluster Quiz
7. Help & TOR Support

### Required Student Actions

1. Open the exact source.
2. Begin at the named location.
3. Read, view, or complete the assigned scope.
4. Attend to chronology, geography, source perspective, evidence, vocabulary, and causal relationships.
5. Record the required notebook evidence.
6. Complete the assigned prompts and independent application.
7. Stop at the named endpoint.
8. Return to Moodle.
9. Submit the required evidence.
10. Complete the Checkpoint and assessment.

### Notebook Requirements

Specify exact ordered items, response count, sentence expectations, source citation, required labels, maps/tables/diagrams, student name, WH-U1-L1, accepted file type, and Moodle destination.

### Evidence Requirement

Moodle is the official evidence record. Savvas submission is also required only when assigned. Preserve every source, map, timeline, image, table, chart, or passage dependency.

### Accessibility and TOR Support

Provide captions/transcripts, alternative text, accessible tables, readable maps, descriptive links, and a direct TOR route for resource or instruction problems.

## Notebook Evidence Submission Specification

### Moodle Activity Title

Notebook Evidence Submission — U1 L1

### Moodle Activity Type

Assignment

### Required Evidence

1. Vocabulary/context/chronology notes.
2. Completed timeline/source-corroboration table.
3. Evidence-based historical explanation.

### Student Submission Instructions

Submit one readable PDF or up to three approved image files. Every page must include student name and WH-U1-L1. Handwritten work is permitted when clearly scanned or photographed.

### Rubric Requirements

Define required evidence, historical accuracy, source use, chronology/geography, reasoning, vocabulary, conventions, full/partial/no-credit performance, common errors, and standards.

### Answer-Key Requirements

The protected TOR guide must include expected content, source evidence, acceptable alternatives, model reasoning, map/chart expectations, misconceptions, and rubric annotations.

### TOR Grading Requirement

The rubric and guide must support consistent scoring without requiring the TOR to reconstruct answers.

## Checkpoint Specification

### Moodle Activity Title

Checkpoint Submission — U1 L1

### Moodle Activity Type

Assignment

### Checkpoint Purpose

Demonstrate independent application through timeline/source-corroboration table and evidence-based historical reasoning.

### Required Student Product

- Completed required visual or source organizer
- Historical claim or explanation
- Relevant evidence and reasoning

### Required Submission

Submit the exact approved evidence in Moodle, labeled with student name and WH-U1-L1.

### Checkpoint Rubric and Answer-Key Requirements

Include expected historical content, required evidence, acceptable variation, reasoning, scoring levels, standard measured, misconceptions, TOR feedback, remediation, and protected exemplar responses.

## Formative Assessment Specification

### Assessment Type

Savvas Lesson Quiz or approved coherent cluster quiz

### Placement

After the independent application and Checkpoint in Lesson 1.1

### Source

Florida Standards and Assessment Overview; Topic 0 Opener; Topic 0 Lessons 1-5 source excerpts and timelines

### Question-Pool Requirement

- Pool size and delivered questions: finalize after item-level inventory of the complete aligned Savvas pool.
- Randomization: only when source and stimulus relationships remain intact.
- Attempts, feedback, and answer release: follow approved MLA policy.
- Mastery requirement: 80%.
- Standards assessed: SS.912.W.1.1, SS.912.W.1.2, SS.912.W.1.3, SS.912.W.1.4, SS.912.W.1.5, SS.912.W.1.6

### Required Stimuli

Preserve each passage, map, timeline, chart, table, political visual, image, or source excerpt with its questions.

## Lesson Completion Standard

The lesson is complete after all pages, Savvas sources, Notebook Tasks, Notebook Evidence, Checkpoint, assessment, completion conditions, and required remediation or revision.

## Lesson Production Notes

Verify Savvas access, source provenance, map and timeline readability, captions/transcripts, alternative text, licensing, teacher-only answer protection, mobile readability, and completion tracking.

## Lesson Identification

| Field | Requirement |

3. Construct an accurate evidence-based historical explanation aligned to SS.912.W.2.14, SS.912.W.2.15, SS.912.W.2.17.

# Unit 2 — Islamic Civilizations, African Kingdoms, and American Civilizations

## Unit Purpose

Analyze Islamic expansion, African empires, and major American civilizations through cultural, political, economic, and geographic evidence.

## Relationship to Prior and Future Units

This unit builds on historical inquiry, chronology, geography, sourcing, and evidence routines established earlier and prepares students for Japan, Renaissance, Reformation, Scientific Revolution, and Exploration.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Explain the unit’s major historical developments with accurate chronology and vocabulary.
2. Analyze required sources, maps, timelines, visuals, and data.
3. Construct evidence-based explanations and demonstrate mastery in the Unit 2 synthesis and assessment.

# Unit 3 — Japan, Renaissance, Reformation, Scientific Revolution, and Exploration

## Unit Purpose

Connect medieval Japan, Renaissance creativity, religious reform, scientific change, exploration, colonization, and forced labor systems.

## Relationship to Prior and Future Units

This unit builds on historical inquiry, chronology, geography, sourcing, and evidence routines established earlier and prepares students for Absolutism, Enlightenment, Revolutions, Industrialization, and Imperialism.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Explain the unit’s major historical developments with accurate chronology and vocabulary.
2. Analyze required sources, maps, timelines, visuals, and data.
3. Construct evidence-based explanations and demonstrate mastery in the Unit 3 synthesis and assessment.

# Unit 4 — Absolutism, Enlightenment, Revolutions, Industrialization, and Imperialism

## Unit Purpose

Analyze political authority, Enlightenment ideas, revolutions, industrialization, reform, nationalism, and imperialism.

## Relationship to Prior and Future Units

This unit builds on historical inquiry, chronology, geography, sourcing, and evidence routines established earlier and prepares students for World War I, Interwar Crisis, World War II, and the Holocaust.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Explain the unit’s major historical developments with accurate chronology and vocabulary.
2. Analyze required sources, maps, timelines, visuals, and data.
3. Construct evidence-based explanations and demonstrate mastery in the Unit 4 synthesis and assessment.

# Unit 5 — World War I, Interwar Crisis, World War II, and the Holocaust

## Unit Purpose

Examine global conflict, authoritarianism, the Holocaust, Allied strategy, atomic warfare, and the consequences of World War II.

## Relationship to Prior and Future Units

This unit builds on historical inquiry, chronology, geography, sourcing, and evidence routines established earlier and prepares students for Cold War, Decolonization, Globalization, Genocide, and Contemporary Issues.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Explain the unit’s major historical developments with accurate chronology and vocabulary.
2. Analyze required sources, maps, timelines, visuals, and data.
3. Construct evidence-based explanations and demonstrate mastery in the Unit 5 synthesis and assessment.

## Unit Standards

# Unit 6 — Cold War, Decolonization, Globalization, Genocide, and Contemporary Issues

## Unit Purpose

Analyze Cold War systems, decolonization, conflict, globalization, terrorism, pandemics, science, and responses to genocide.

## Relationship to Prior and Future Units

This unit builds on historical inquiry, chronology, geography, sourcing, and evidence routines established earlier and prepares students for course synthesis and contemporary historical reasoning.

## Unit Learning Objectives

By the end of this unit, students will be able to:

1. Explain the unit’s major historical developments with accurate chronology and vocabulary.
2. Analyze required sources, maps, timelines, visuals, and data.
3. Construct evidence-based explanations and demonstrate mastery in the Unit 6 synthesis and assessment.
